11:["$","p",null,{"style":{"fontSize":"1rem","color":"var(--text-body)","lineHeight":1.75,"marginBottom":"1.25em","marginTop":0},"children":"Flow matching (Lipman et al., 2022; Liu et al., 2022; Albergo and Vanden-Eijnden, 2022) reframes generative modeling as regression on a vector field rather than denoising at each noise level. This reformulation is simpler to derive, produces straighter trajectories (fewer function evaluations at inference), and generalizes naturally to any source-target pair rather than requiring a Gaussian source. Consistency models (Song et al., 2023) take a further step: instead of learning a multi-step process, they learn a function that maps any point on a diffusion trajectory directly to the clean sample in a single evaluation. Together, these methods represent the frontier of fast generative inference."}]

OT-CFM uses a mini-batch optimal transport coupling to make trajectories straighter, reducing the number of inference steps. The Sinkhorn algorithm solves regularized OT within each batch in ","$L192"," time with minimal wall-clock overhead. Stochastic interpolants unify flow matching and DDPM as special cases of a general probability path framework. Consistency models learn the endpoint mapping ","$L193"," along a diffusion trajectory, enabling single-step generation; consistency distillation trains the model against a teacher ODE solver, while consistency training uses progressive time discretization for training from scratch."]}]
